# Skills · C · part 254

854 entries, court-jester to coverage-check. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **court-jester**: Use when the user wants a plan, proposal, or conclusion adversarially challenged in one pass — auto-picks the strongest critique mode (socratic, dialectic, pre-mortem, red-team, evidence audit) and delivers the 3–5 strongest challenges plus a synthesis. Triggers on: "court-jester this", "poke holes in this plan", "red-team this design", "run a pre-mortem", "argue the other side", "does the evidence actually support this conclusion", "challenge this proposal". ([dekoza/oh-my-slop](https://github.com/dekoza/oh-my-slop/tree/HEAD/skills/workflow/court-jester) · ★4)
- **court-judgment-prediction-eval**: Tests a model's ability to predict binary case outcomes (accepted/denied) and generate human-readable explanations by citing relevant sentences from the input document. This probes joint reasoning, outcome forecasting, and justification generation in legal contexts. Use when the user wants to benchmark on LegalEval CJPE Dataset, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports standard F1 score. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/court-judgment-prediction-eval) · ★3)
- **court-municipal-license-clerk**: Use when a task needs the judgment of a court, municipal, or license clerk — checking a pro se filing for procedural completeness, calculating a court filing fee or fee-waiver eligibility, processing a license or permit application against an eligibility checklist, determining whether a case record is public or sealed, or making a docket entry. - **covariance**: Used by: the covariance agent (stage 3), the Black-Litterman CMA method (implied returns need a covariance matrix), every portfolio-construction agent, and the CRO agent (ex-ante volatility and tracking error). Paper reference: Ang, Azimbayev & Kim (2026) §3.1 step 3 — "A covariance agent estimates the asset class covariance matrix using historical data and macro forecasts." ([SajalGoyla/agentic-strategic-asset-allocation](https://github.com/SajalGoyla/agentic-strategic-asset-allocation/tree/HEAD/src/saa/skills/covariance))
- **covariance-and-risk-models**: Estimate a covariance matrix an optimizer can actually invert, and report how much variance it hides. TRIGGER - covariance matrix estimation, sample covariance singular, "matrix is not positive definite", np.cov more assets than observations, N &gt; T, condition number, Ledoit-Wolf shrinkage, sklearn LedoitWolf, CovarianceShrinkage, shrinkage intensity or delta, RiskMetrics EWMA, lambda 0.94 or 0.97, exponentially weighted covariance, exp_cov span, PCA or statistical factor risk model, Marchenko-Pastur, Barra fundamental factor model, specific risk, predicted vs realized volatility, risk model b… ([howard-lynn-ye/Fin-RSI](https://github.com/howard-lynn-ye/Fin-RSI/tree/HEAD/fin_skills/_skills/covariance-and-risk-models) · ★1 · 4 copies)
- **covariance-builder**: Full covariance matrix estimation methodology using four estimators — Ledoit-Wolf (primary), EWMA, crisis-regime, and normal-regime — for all 18 asset classes. ([AltbridgeInc/apex-plugin-analysis-assetclass](https://github.com/AltbridgeInc/apex-plugin-analysis-assetclass/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/covariance-builder))
- **covariance-matrix-computation**: Use when after normalization (Step 7) is complete and you have a clean feature matrix ready for multivariate statistical analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/covariance-matrix-computation) · 2 copies)
- **covariance-matrix-estimation-from-time-course-metabolomics**: Use when metabolomic abundance data have been split into condition subsets (e.g. 6C stress vs 16C control) and a covariance matrix is needed as input to calculate_jacobian for inferring metabolite interaction networks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariance-matrix-estimation-from-time-course-metabolomics))
- **covariance-matrix-inversion**: Use when after generating a covariance matrix from normalized metabolite abundance data in MetaboAnalyst, and before performing network-level metabolomic inference or visualizing metabolite interaction networks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/covariance-matrix-inversion))
- **covariance-matrix-io-for-network-inference**: Use when metabolite data has already been condition-prepared with MInfer's prepare_data and a per-condition covariance matrix must be generated and exported as the cov argument for calculate_jacobian. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariance-matrix-io-for-network-inference))
- **covariance-matrix-svd-preservation**: Use when a missing-value imputation benchmark needs a complete simulated metabolomics data matrix with realistic covariance and group structure, so MNAR patterns can be injected and imputation accuracy be measured against known truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariance-matrix-svd-preservation))
- **covariance-model-alignment-gap-analysis**: Use when screening assembled or database 16S rRNA gene sequences (e.g. SILVA 115 records or metagenome assemblies) for large insertion sequences, introns, or insertion-encoded ORFs whose boundaries must be located relative to a structural rRNA alignment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariance-model-alignment-gap-analysis))
- **covariance-moment-based-network-inference**: Use when you have time-stamped cross-sectional single-cell expression data (qPCR or RNA-seq) from two consecutive time points and need to infer a sparse, directed, signed transition matrix A_t without constructing pseudo-time trajectories. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariance-moment-based-network-inference))
- **covariance-restricted-sparse-linear-regression**: Use when you have TSCS single-cell expression data (qPCR or RNA-seq) sampled at consecutive time points and need to infer the transition-driving GRN without pseudo-time trajectory construction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariance-restricted-sparse-linear-regression))
- **covariance-spectrum-decomposition-single-cell**: Use when a single-cell count matrix is heavily sparse (e.g. ~95% zeros) and you need to quantify how much of the covariance eigen-spectrum departs from Marchenko-Pastur/Tracy-Widom expectations and partition that departure into sparsity artifacts versus biological signal. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariance-spectrum-decomposition-single-cell))
- **covariance-spectrum-eigendecomposition-analysis**: Use when a sparse cell-by-gene expression matrix has been eigendecomposed and you must decide which eigenvalue/eigenvector pairs are noise, sparsity artifacts, or true biological signal — including discarding delocalized eigenvectors whose eigenvalues fall outside the MP bulk. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariance-spectrum-eigendecomposition-analysis))
- **covariant-fibrations**: This page used to hold an archived copy of this skill. The archive now lives in the registry's data repository. ([majiayu000/claude-skill-registry](https://github.com/majiayu000/claude-skill-registry/tree/HEAD/skills/ai-ml/covariant-fibrations) · ★745)
- **covariant-fibrations**: Riehl-Shulman covariant fibrations for dependent types over directed ([plurigrid/asi](https://github.com/plurigrid/asi/tree/HEAD/plugins/asi/skills/covariant-fibrations) · ★72 · 3 copies)
- **covariant-fibrations**: Riehl-Shulman covariant fibrations for dependent types over directed intervals in synthetic ∞-categories. ([plurigrid/asi](https://github.com/plurigrid/asi/tree/HEAD/skills/covariant-fibrations) · ★72 · 3 copies)
- **covariant-modification**: Unified skill modification with covariant transport, Darwin Gödel Machine evolution, and MCP Tasks self-rewriting. GF(3) conserved. ([plurigrid/asi](https://github.com/plurigrid/asi/tree/HEAD/skills/covariant-modification) · ★72 · 2 copies)
- **covariant-qec-quantum-brain**: Covariant quantum error correction methodology for quantum brain models. Evaluates CQEC purification protocols across radical-pair proteins with ab initio spin Hamiltonians, analyzing layer-specific coherence dynamics and T2 sensitivity. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/neuroscience/covariant-qec-quantum-brain) · ★4 · 2 copies)
- **covariant-quantum-codes**: SU(d)-covariant approximate quantum codes for protected analog computation with Theta(1/N) error scaling and Petz recovery map decoder, enabling continuous symmetry-preserving quantum error correction. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/quantum/covariant-quantum-codes) · ★4)
- **covariate-adjusted-genotype-association-testing**: Use when genotype data and transcript expression (e.g., GTEx V7 per-tissue) are available and the goal is to detect variants affecting the global splicing pattern of genes rather than single univariate splicing traits. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-adjusted-genotype-association-testing))
- **covariate-adjusted-glm-modeling**: Use when performing transcriptomics differential expression testing on RNA-seq count matrices where sample metadata contains confounders (e.g. CONDITION, SEX, AGE columns) that must be included in the design formula before filtering significant genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-glm-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-linear-modeling-ancova**: Use when a metabolomics or proteomics feature table has study covariates (metadata beyond the primary group label) and you need to test whether covariate adjustment changes which features are statistically significant versus plain ANOVA/t-tests. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-linear-modeling-ancova))
- **covariate-adjusted-linear-regression**: Use when comparing treatment groups on a continuous microbiome outcome (alpha-diversity index or CLR-transformed abundance) while adjusting for covariates such as Sex, as in MiSurv Module 2. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-linear-regression))
- **covariate-adjusted-linear-regression-fdr**: Use when you have per-cell module-specific expression scores from a GRN (e.g. SCING Leiden modules scored with AUCell) plus per-cell clinical metadata (trait labels and confounders such as sex), and need FDR-controlled module-trait association statistics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-adjusted-linear-regression-fdr))
- **covariate-adjusted-lipidomics-statistics**: Use when a lipidomics dataset (e.g. Metabolomics Workbench study ST001111) has loaded clinical variables and the differential-abundance contrast may be confounded by a covariate such as Race, requiring a factorial model like ~ Race + SampleType. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-lipidomics-statistics))
- **covariate-adjusted-longitudinal-modeling**: Use when the input is a longitudinal master table (features x samples over repeated time points, e.g. genus-level fasting stool data with a Day or Time_point variable) and time effects must be separated from covariate effects in count, proportion, binary, ordinal or continuous data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-longitudinal-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-mirkat-s-analysis**: Use when you have an OTU/feature table with matched survival times, censoring/event status, and covariates, and need a kernel-based beta-diversity-vs-survival association test (MiRKAT-S), optionally within a treatment-group subgroup. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-mirkat-s-analysis))
- **covariate-adjusted-prediction-modeling**: Use when the goal is a supervised classification model (e.g., breast cancer vs. control) from metabolomics and/or gene-expression data where covariate adjustment and feature filtering must be demonstrated against reference metrics like Q2Y = 0.701. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjusted-prediction-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-regression-modeling**: Use when testing host genetic variants (SNVs/SNPs) for association with microbial taxon abundances across a sample cohort and covariates (e.g., covariates.txt/CovFile) must be adjusted for in the regression model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-adjusted-regression-modeling))
- **covariate-adjusted-spatial-modeling**: Use when analyzing spatially-resolved transcriptomics data (e.g., SpatialExperiment objects) where known spatial domain labels exist and you need to detect SVGs adjusted for domain structure, or benchmark SVG detection against SPARK-X, HVGs, and Moran's I. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-adjusted-spatial-modeling))
- **covariate-adjustment-confounder-control**: Use when scanning ~249 NMR metabolic biomarkers (plus derived ratios) for associations with incident CAD or stroke in UK Biobank, and results must be adjusted for age and sex with prevalent cases and lipid-lowering medication users excluded. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjustment-confounder-control))
- **covariate-adjustment-via-linear-regression-residuals**: Use when raw metabolomics peak intensities (e.g., Metabolomics Workbench ST001386/ST001636 TEDDY data) contain demographic covariates such as age and sex that confound a case/control contrast, before creating the DNEA object. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-adjustment-via-linear-regression-residuals))
- **covariate-association-agreement-testing**: Use when validating quality of feature matches between two untargeted LC-MS metabolomics datasets by checking whether matched feature pairs carry consistent covariate associations (age, BMI, gender) in both datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/covariate-association-agreement-testing))
- **covariate-confounding-benchmark-design**: Use when benchmarking longitudinal microbiome differential-abundance tools under covariate-time confounding, or when deciding whether a tool's FDR/TPR is robust to increasing numbers of dummy covariates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-confounding-benchmark-design))
- **covariate-effect-success-rate-evaluation**: Use when comparing longitudinal differential-abundance tools (e.g. LongDat vs MaAsLin2) on simulated multi-covariate data and you need to quantify whether covariate control keeps FDR stable and covariate-effect success rate near 0.95. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-effect-success-rate-evaluation))
- **covariate-encoded-trajectory-modelling**: Use when a cross-sectional single-cell expression cohort carries a sample-level covariate (stimulant, mutation, drug) that may modulate progression, and pseudotime must be inferred without explicit time-series labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-encoded-trajectory-modelling))
- **covariate-gene-interaction-significance-testing**: Use when a cross-sectional expression cohort (e.g. TCGA-BRCA log2(TPM+1) matrix of 4579 HVGs x 1135 samples) must be ordered along a pseudotemporal trajectory while testing which genes' loadings interact with a sample covariate such as ER status. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-gene-interaction-significance-testing))
- **covariate-priors**: Record each candidate parameter–covariate relationship, its rationale, the functional form and the planned evaluation before searching. Freeze the prior list and disclose deviations for local review. Keep a trail of tested candidates, model comparisons and decisions. ([MahmAbdelwahab/pmx-harness-public](https://github.com/MahmAbdelwahab/pmx-harness-public/tree/HEAD/skills/covariate-priors))
- **covariate-priors**: Use when building or reviewing a covariate model: the SME physiological prior list is frozen before any search, and the search (SCM, FREM, or prior-driven full model) runs only within it — the countermeasure to OFV-chasing covariate work. ([MahmAbdelwahab/pmx-harness-public](https://github.com/MahmAbdelwahab/pmx-harness-public/tree/HEAD/.github/skills/covariate-priors))
- **covariate-pseudotime-interaction-ranking**: Use when a PhenoPath fit is available and the goal is to rank genes by covariate-pseudotime interaction effect size and identify significant interactions (e.g. Shalek LPS/PAM genes, TCGA MSI analysis). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-pseudotime-interaction-ranking))
- **covariate-reducibility-classification**: Use when analyzing longitudinal high-dimensional data (e.g. gut microbial abundance over Day or Time_point) where a significant time effect on a feature must be checked against potentially confounding covariates before being declared a treatment effect. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-reducibility-classification))
- **covariate-regression-from-gene-expression**: Use when comparing the wall-clock scalability of nuisance-covariate removal methods (SiFT, scVI, scanpy regress_out) on large scRNA-seq data, e.g. continuous covariates regressed from the expression matrix across subsamples up to ~500,000 cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-regression-from-gene-expression))
- **covariate-regression-on-sample-parameters**: Use when you have a fitted GEDI model for a multi-sample single-cell dataset (e.g., PBMCsca) and need to visualize sample-level manifold parameters or verify that GEDI disentangled donor from technology effects, as in Fig. 2a/2d UMAPs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-regression-on-sample-parameters))
- **covariate-regression-residualization-limma**: Use when a per-cell technical covariate (e.g. dsb's PC1 of isotype controls and fitted Gaussian-mixture background means µ1) must be regressed out of normalized protein/ADT values to return denoised residuals. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/covariate-regression-residualization-limma))
- **covariate-taxon-association-extraction-from-mppi**: Use when you have MCMC posterior samples from a Dirichlet-multinomial (DM) regression fit in MicroBVS and need to declare which covariate-taxon associations are selected, e.g. against a simulated ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/covariate-taxon-association-extraction-from-mppi))
- **covariates**: Interview-based covariate selection for DiD designs. Walks the user through 5 questions ONE AT A TIME, then synthesizes the "10-chapter book by the world's leading expert on Y(0) trends" — those 10 chapters are the covariates. Grounded in Heckman, Ichimura & Todd (1997, RESTUD) approach: find the X that drives E[Y(0)] trends. Then suggests data sources and fetches the data once confirmed. ([scunning1975/mixtape_harness](https://github.com/scunning1975/mixtape_harness/tree/HEAD/skills/covariates) · ★16)
- **covarity-brand**: Apply the Covarity brand system to product interfaces, documentation, repositories, and generated assets. ([shruggietech/shruggie-brand](https://github.com/shruggietech/shruggie-brand/tree/HEAD/brands/covarity) · ★1)
- **covate-review**: Spaced review of what the user learned. Use when the user wants a quiz on recent work, asks what to review today, or wants their answers from a review page scored. ([SunflowersLwtech/covate](https://github.com/SunflowersLwtech/covate/tree/HEAD/plugins/covate/skills/covate-review) · ★8)

- **cove**: Spin up and manage local WordPress (and static) sites with the Cove CLI — automatic HTTPS, one-click admin login, shared MariaDB, Mailpit, no Docker. - **cove**: Apply Chain-of-Verification to reduce hallucinations. Use when accuracy matters - complex questions, factual claims, research. Invoked as /cove [question]. ([moszoro/skills](https://github.com/moszoro/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cove))
- **cove**: Agentic Chain-of-Verification (CoVe 2.0) — open-book, tool-interactive self-verification. Manually triggered via /cove to verify and refine a response through a three-phase pipeline (adaptive draft & plan → tiered open-book verification → critique & finalize with citations). Grafts CRITIC's external-tool verification onto CoVe's structured deliberation. Ideal for fact-heavy answers where accuracy is critical. ([RayChang/agent-skills](https://github.com/RayChang/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/cove))
- **cove**: Apply Chain-of-Verification (CoVe) prompting to improve response accuracy through self-verification. Use when complex questions require fact-checking, technical accuracy, or multi-step reasoning. ([shivamchopra7/claude-skill-directory](https://github.com/shivamchopra7/claude-skill-directory/tree/HEAD/skills/ai-llm/cove-serpro69-claude-toolbox) · 3 copies)
- **Cove API Patterns**: How the Cove Data Protection MCP tools talk to N-able's JSON-RPC API through Conduit: the three X-Cove-* headers and the API user behind them, the 15-minute visa session, finding partner (customer) IDs, statistics column codes and units, paging and truncation, Cove error numbers, and which tool is admin-gated in Conduit. ([WYRE-AI/msp-claude-plugins](https://github.com/WYRE-AI/msp-claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/msp-claude-plugins/cove/cove/skills/api-patterns) · ★55)
- **Cove Backup Health Triage**: Finding Cove devices whose backups failed, finished with errors, or have gone stale, for one customer or the whole customer tree, using cove_device_statistics_list column queries; classifying each device; drilling into a device's profile and data sources; and writing a ranked triage list a technician can work through. ([WYRE-AI/msp-claude-plugins](https://github.com/WYRE-AI/msp-claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/msp-claude-plugins/cove/cove/skills/backup-health) · ★55)
- **Cove Console Access Review**: Reviewing who can sign in to the Cove Management Console for each customer and with which role, spotting leavers and over-privileged accounts, and reading the user-action audit log (cove_audit_actions_list, admin-gated in Conduit and needing an Administrator API user in Cove) to see who changed devices, retention, users or customers. ([WYRE-AI/msp-claude-plugins](https://github.com/WYRE-AI/msp-claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/msp-claude-plugins/cove/cove/skills/access-review) · ★55)
- **Cove Customer and Storage Inventory**: Building a per-customer inventory from Cove: the customer (partner) tree and each customer's state, assigned products and retention, protected device counts, storage used and its growth, and the storage locations that hold the data.
